Pancetta, from pancia, belly, is one of the most used cured meats in Italian home cooking. The flat version, pancetta tesa, is the everyday kitchen one: it is cut into cubes or strips and cooked, giving flavour to countless sauces, soups and vegetable dishes across the country.

### How it is made
Pork belly is trimmed into a neat slab, then rubbed with salt and aromatic seasoning, which is what aromatizzata refers to. After resting so the salt can penetrate, it is hung flat to dry and age for several weeks. Unlike rolled pancetta, it keeps its natural layers of meat and fat side by side.

### Curiosities
- Tesa means stretched or flat, as opposed to arrotolata, rolled.
- In many regions pancetta is the fat that starts a soffritto, together with onion, carrot and celery.
- Outside Lazio, pancetta is often used in place of guanciale in carbonara and amatriciana.

### How to enjoy it
Cut it into cubes or strips and brown it slowly in a pan to start a pasta sauce, a risotto or a pasta e fagioli. Wrap slices around vegetables or meat before roasting, or crisp thin slices in the oven to crumble over salads.123:T4b0,### Waar het vandaan komt
Pancetta, van pancia, buik, is een van de meest gebruikte vleeswaren in de Italiaanse thuiskeuken. De platte versie, pancetta tesa, is die van elke dag: in blokjes of reepjes gesneden en gebakken geeft hij smaak aan talloze sauzen, soepen en groentegerechten in het hele land.

### Hoe het gemaakt wordt
Varkensbuik wordt tot een nette plak bijgesneden en ingewreven met zout en aromatische kruiding, waar aromatizzata naar verwijst. Na het rusten, zodat het zout kan intrekken, wordt hij plat te drogen en rijpen gehangen, enkele weken lang. Anders dan opgerolde pancetta houdt hij zijn natuurlijke lagen vlees en vet naast elkaar.

### Weetjes
- Tesa betekent uitgestrekt of plat, tegenover arrotolata, opgerold.
- In veel regio's is pancetta het vet waarmee een soffritto begint, samen met ui, wortel en bleekselderij.
- Buiten Lazio gebruikt men vaak pancetta in plaats van guanciale in carbonara en amatriciana.

### Zo geniet je ervan
Snijd hem in blokjes of reepjes en bak hem langzaam aan als begin van een pastasaus, een risotto of pasta e fagioli. Wikkel plakjes om groenten of vlees voor in de oven, of bak dunne plakjes knapperig om over salades te verkruimelen.124:T485,### Da dove viene
La pancetta, da pancia, è uno dei salumi più usati nella cucina di casa italiana. La versione tesa è quella di tutti i giorni: tagliata a cubetti o listarelle e cotta, dà sapore a innumerevoli sughi, minestre e piatti di verdure in tutto il Paese.

### Come si fa
La pancia di maiale viene rifilata in una lastra regolare, poi massaggiata con sale e aromi, da cui il nome aromatizzata. Dopo il riposo, perché il sale penetri, viene appesa distesa ad asciugare e stagionare per alcune settimane. A differenza di quella arrotolata, conserva gli strati naturali di carne e grasso uno accanto all'altro.

### Curiosità
- Tesa vuol dire distesa, al contrario di arrotolata.
- In molte regioni è la pancetta il grasso con cui parte il soffritto, insieme a cipolla, carota e sedano.
- Fuori dal Lazio si usa spesso la pancetta al posto del guanciale in carbonara e amatriciana.

### Come gustarlo
Tagliala a cubetti o listarelle e rosolala piano per iniziare un sugo, un risotto o una pasta e fagioli. Avvolgi le fette intorno a verdure o carne prima di infornarle, oppure fai croccare fette sottili in forno da sbriciolare sulle insalate.125:T4fa,### Where it comes from
Capocollo takes its name from capo and collo, head and neck, because it comes from the muscle between the pig's head and shoulder. It is a classic of southern Italy: Calabria has a protected capocollo, and Puglia, Campania and Basilicata have their own well-known versions. In the north the same cut is usually called coppa.

### How it is made
The whole neck muscle is trimmed, salted and seasoned, then left to rest so the salt can work its way in. It is pressed into a casing, tied and hung to age for months. Because it is a whole muscle rather than ground meat, every slice shows the natural marbling of fat through the deep red meat. Dolce means this is the mild style, not the spicy one.

### Curiosities
- Coppa and capocollo are the same cut; the name changes as you travel from north to south.
- The fine streaks of fat are what keep capocollo tender and fragrant after long ageing.
- In the south, capocollo is traditionally made during the winter pig-killing season and eaten through the following months.

### How to enjoy it
Slice it thin and let it come to room temperature. Serve it on a board with bread, olives and cheese, or in a panino with provola or mozzarella. It also works on pizza straight from the oven, added after baking.126:T4ff,### Waar het vandaan komt
Capocollo dankt zijn naam aan capo en collo, kop en nek, omdat het van de spier tussen kop en schouder van het varken komt. Het is een klassieker uit Zuid-Italië: Calabrië heeft een beschermde capocollo, en Apulië, Campanië en Basilicata hebben hun eigen bekende versies. In het noorden heet hetzelfde stuk meestal coppa.

### Hoe het gemaakt wordt
De hele nekspier wordt bijgesneden, gezouten en gekruid en daarna laten rusten zodat het zout kan intrekken. Hij wordt in een darm gedrukt, opgebonden en maandenlang te rijpen gehangen. - Coppa en capocollo zijn hetzelfde stuk; de naam verandert als je van noord naar zuid reist.
- De fijne vetadertjes houden capocollo mals en geurig, ook na lang rijpen.
- In het zuiden wordt capocollo traditioneel in de winterse slachttijd gemaakt en de maanden daarna gegeten.

Il capocollo prende il nome da capo e collo, perché si ricava dal muscolo tra la testa e la spalla del maiale. È un classico del Sud: la Calabria ha un capocollo a denominazione protetta, e Puglia, Campania e Basilicata hanno le loro versioni famose. Al Nord lo stesso taglio si chiama di solito coppa.

Pancetta takes its name from pancia, belly. It is made all over Italy, and almost every region has its own way of seasoning it. There are two main shapes: tesa, left flat, and arrotolata, rolled up tight. Rolled pancetta is the one most often sliced thin and eaten as it is.

### How it is made
Pork belly is trimmed, the rind is usually removed, and the meat is rubbed with salt and seasoning. After resting, it is rolled into a tight cylinder, tied or pressed into a casing, and hung to age for weeks or months. Dolce means it is the mild style, without chilli heat.

### Curiosities
- Rolling the belly is what gives each slice its spiral of pink meat and white fat.
- Flat pancetta is mostly for cooking, while rolled pancetta is more often served raw, thinly sliced.
- Pancetta is a common base for soffritto, the gentle start of many Italian sauces and soups.

### How to enjoy it

Pancetta dankt zijn naam aan pancia, buik. Het wordt in heel Italië gemaakt, en bijna elke regio heeft een eigen manier van kruiden. Er zijn twee hoofdvormen: tesa, plat gelaten, en arrotolata, strak opgerold. Opgerolde pancetta wordt het vaakst dun gesneden en zo gegeten.

### Hoe het gemaakt wordt
Varkensbuik wordt bijgesneden, meestal zonder zwoerd, en ingewreven met zout en kruiden. Na het rusten wordt hij strak opgerold, opgebonden of in een darm gedrukt, en weken tot maanden te rijpen gehangen. Dolce betekent dat het de milde stijl is, zonder pepers.

- Door het oprollen krijgt elke plak een spiraal van roze vlees en wit vet.
- Platte pancetta is vooral om mee te koken, opgerolde pancetta wordt vaker rauw en dun gesneden gegeten.
- Pancetta is vaak de basis van soffritto, het rustige begin van veel Italiaanse sauzen en soepen.

Pancetta viene da pancia. Si produce in tutta Italia e quasi ogni regione ha il suo modo di aromatizzarla. Le forme principali sono due: tesa, lasciata piatta, e arrotolata, avvolta stretta su sé stessa. La pancetta arrotolata è quella che più spesso si affetta sottile e si mangia così.

La pancia di maiale viene rifilata, di solito privata della cotenna, e massaggiata con sale e aromi. Dopo il riposo viene arrotolata a cilindro, legata o insaccata e appesa a stagionare per settimane o mesi. Dolce vuol dire che è la versione non piccante.

Mortadella is the great cold cut of Bologna, the capital of Emilia-Romagna, a city nicknamed la grassa, the fat one, for its rich cooking. Mortadella Bologna is a protected geographical indication, and the version with pistachio is one of the styles its rules allow.

### How it is made
Pork is ground very finely into a smooth paste, then mixed with small cubes of fat, called lardelli, and seasoning. In this version pistachios are added too. The mixture is stuffed into a casing and cooked slowly for many hours in dry-air ovens, until it is cooked all the way through the large, round loaf.

### Curiosities
- The name is often traced to the Latin for myrtle, once used to flavour it, or to the mortar used to grind the meat.
- The American word baloney comes from Bologna, though the Italian original is quite different.
- The small white cubes of fat are what give each slice its typical look and silky feel.

Water buffalo have been raised for centuries in the flat, wet lands of Campania, around Caserta and Salerno, and in southern Lazio. Their milk is richer than cow's milk, and the region turned it into its most famous fresh cheese: mozzarella di bufala.

### How it is made
The milk is curdled, the curd is cut and left to rest until it is ready to stretch. It is then worked in very hot water until it becomes smooth and elastic, a technique called pasta filata. Finally the cheese is shaped by hand or machine and pinched off into pieces, then kept in a light brine that protects it.

### Curiosities
- The name comes from mozzare, to cut off: the cheesemaker tears each piece from the stretched curd.
- A good buffalo mozzarella should give off a little milky liquid when you cut it.
- It is at its best within a few days, which is why it is rarely aged.

### How to enjoy it
Take it out of the fridge about half an hour before eating so the texture softens and the flavour opens up. Tear it rather than slicing it. Serve with ripe tomatoes, basil, good olive oil and bread, or add it to a warm pasta at the very last moment.12f:T4a4,### Waar het vandaan komt
Waterbuffels worden al eeuwenlang gehouden in de vlakke, natte gebieden van Campanië, rond Caserta en Salerno, en in het zuiden van Lazio. Hun melk is voller dan koemelk, en de streek maakte er haar beroemdste verse kaas van: mozzarella di bufala.

### Hoe het gemaakt wordt
De melk wordt gestremd, de wrongel gesneden en gerust totdat hij klaar is om te trekken. Daarna wordt hij in heel heet water gekneed tot hij glad en elastisch is, een techniek die pasta filata heet. Tot slot wordt de kaas met de hand of machinaal gevormd, in stukken afgeknepen en bewaard in een lichte pekel die hem beschermt.

### Weetjes
- De naam komt van mozzare, afknippen: de kaasmaker scheurt elk stuk van de getrokken wrongel af.
- Een goede buffelmozzarella geeft bij het aansnijden een beetje melkachtig vocht af.
- Hij is het lekkerst binnen een paar dagen; daarom wordt hij zelden gerijpt.

### Zo geniet je ervan
Haal hem ongeveer een halfuur voor het eten uit de koelkast, dan wordt de structuur zachter en komt de smaak vrij. Scheur hem liever dan te snijden. Lekker met rijpe tomaten, basilicum, goede olijfolie en brood, of op het allerlaatste moment door warme pasta.130:T496,### Da dove viene
Le bufale si allevano da secoli nelle terre pianeggianti e umide della Campania, intorno a Caserta e Salerno, e nel basso Lazio. Il loro latte è più ricco di quello vaccino e il territorio ne ha fatto il suo formaggio fresco più famoso: la mozzarella di bufala.

### Come si fa
Il latte viene cagliato, la cagliata tagliata e lasciata riposare finché non è pronta per la filatura. Alla fine la mozzarella viene formata a mano o a macchina, mozzata in pezzi e conservata in un liquido leggermente salato che la protegge.

- Una buona mozzarella di bufala rilascia un po' di liquido lattiginoso quando la tagli.
- È al meglio entro pochi giorni, ed è per questo che raramente si stagiona.

### Come gustarlo
Tirala fuori dal frigo una mezz'ora prima, così la consistenza si ammorbidisce e il sapore si apre. Meglio strapparla che affettarla. Servila con pomodori maturi, basilico, buon olio e pane, oppure aggiungila alla pasta calda all'ultimo momento.131:T463,### Where it comes from
Pecorino comes from pecora, sheep. Pecorino Romano is one of Italy's oldest cheeses and today a protected designation of origin. It may be made only in Lazio, in Sardinia and in the province of Grosseto in Tuscany, and most of it now comes from Sardinia.

### How it is made
It is made from sheep's milk, which is curdled, cut into small grains and pressed into large wheels. The wheels are salted, traditionally by hand with dry salt, over several weeks, then aged for months. The long salting is what gives Pecorino Romano its firm body and its bold, salty taste.

### Curiosities
- Pecorino was part of the rations of Roman soldiers, as it kept well on long marches.
- In Rome it is traditional to eat fresh fava beans with pecorino in spring, especially on 1 May.
- Its saltiness is the reason Roman pasta recipes often need little or no extra salt.

### How to enjoy it
Grate it finely over pasta: it is essential in cacio e pepe, carbonara, gricia and amatriciana. Shave it over salads or roasted vegetables, or serve small chunks with honey, pears or fresh fava beans and a glass of red wine.132:T4b0,### Waar het vandaan komt
Pecorino komt van pecora, schaap. Pecorino Romano is een van de oudste kazen van Italië en heeft nu een beschermde oorsprongsbenaming. Hij mag alleen gemaakt worden in Lazio, op Sardinië en in de provincie Grosseto in Toscane, en het grootste deel komt tegenwoordig van Sardinië.

### Hoe het gemaakt wordt
Hij wordt gemaakt van schapenmelk, die wordt gestremd, in kleine korrels gesneden en in grote wielen geperst. De wielen worden een aantal weken gezouten, traditioneel met de hand met droog zout, en daarna maandenlang gerijpt. Door dat lange zouten krijgt Pecorino Romano zijn stevige structuur en zijn uitgesproken, zoute smaak.

### Weetjes
- Pecorino hoorde bij de rantsoenen van Romeinse soldaten, omdat hij lang goed bleef op marsen.

- Door het zout heb je bij Romeinse pastarecepten vaak weinig of geen extra zout nodig.

Lucanica takes its name from Lucania, the ancient name of today's Basilicata in southern Italy. It is one of the oldest documented sausages in Italian food history: Roman writers such as Varro and the cookbook attributed to Apicius already mention it, and Varro wrote that the Romans learned it from soldiers from Lucania.

### How it is made
Pork is minced, seasoned and stuffed into a natural casing, then hung to dry and cure. The aged version, like this one, loses moisture over the weeks and becomes firm enough to slice like a salame. The piccante style is noticeably spicy, as is common in southern Italian cured meats.

### Curiosities
- The Roman name spread across Italy: the northern luganega sausage takes its name from the same root.
- Lucanica is often shaped as a long, thin sausage, sometimes curved into a horseshoe.
- In Basilicata cured sausages are part of the traditional family pig-killing season in winter.

### How to enjoy it

Spianata means flattened, and that is exactly what it looks like. The spicy version is best known as a speciality of Calabria, the toe of Italy, a region famous for its love of chilli heat. It belongs to the same family of southern cured meats as soppressata, capocollo and 'nduja.

### How it is made
Pork is ground, seasoned and stuffed into a casing, then pressed flat before it is hung to dry and age. The pressing gives the salame its long, wide shape and its broad, oval slices. The piccante style owes its heat and warm red colour to chilli, which is typical of Calabrian salumi.

### Curiosities
- Calabria is so tied to chilli that the region celebrates it with a festival, and many dishes are finished with it.
- The flat shape helps the salame dry evenly, since heat and air reach the centre more easily.
- Outside Calabria, spianata is also made in a milder Roman style.

### How to enjoy it

Hij hoort bij dezelfde familie zuidelijke vleeswaren als soppressata, capocollo en 'nduja.

- Door de platte vorm droogt de salami gelijkmatiger, omdat lucht de kern makkelijker bereikt.
- Buiten Calabrië bestaat spianata ook in een mildere Romeinse stijl.

In Italy the word prosciutto on its own usually means the dry-cured kind, so the cooked version always carries its second name: cotto. It is made across the country, with a strong tradition in the north, and it is one of the most eaten cold cuts in Italian homes, bars and school lunch boxes.

### How it is made
A whole pork leg is deboned and trimmed, then treated with a seasoned brine. The meat rests so the flavour can spread, is pressed into a mould to give it its shape, and is then cooked slowly at a gentle temperature. After cooling it is ready to slice: pale pink, tender and mild.

### Curiosities
- Ask for prosciutto in an Italian shop and you will be offered crudo; ask for cotto if you want this one.
- The toast prosciutto e formaggio, a toasted sandwich of cooked ham and cheese, is a fixture of Italian bars.
- It is one of the first cold cuts many Italian children eat, thanks to its mild flavour.

### How to enjoy it

Hij wordt in het hele land gemaakt, met een sterke traditie in het noorden, en is een van de meest gegeten vleeswaren in Italiaanse keukens, bars en schooltassen.

San Daniele del Friuli is a hill town in Friuli Venezia Giulia, in the north-east of Italy. Prosciutto di San Daniele is a protected designation of origin, and the town's hams have been known for centuries. Local producers credit the climate: cold air coming down from the Alps meets milder air rising from the Adriatic, and the river Tagliamento runs just below the hills.

### How it is made
Fresh pork legs are salted with sea salt, pressed, rested and then left to age slowly in ventilated rooms. The rules of the designation allow nothing but the leg and salt, and the ham must age for at least thirteen months before it can carry the name. Time does the rest, turning the meat soft, rosy and sweet.

### Curiosities
- A San Daniele keeps its trotter, which gives the whole ham its typical guitar shape.
- Each approved ham is branded on the rind with the designation's mark.
- Pressing during salting is what gives the ham its compact, flattened form.

### How to enjoy it

Agerola is a mountain town in the Monti Lattari, high above the Amalfi Coast in Campania. It has a long dairy tradition, and the area is known for its cheeses, from fresh fior di latte to aged provolone. Provola is one of the everyday cheeses of this landscape.

### How it is made
Provola is a pasta filata cheese, a close relative of mozzarella. The curd is stretched in hot water and shaped into a round, but it is left to firm up and dry a little, so it is less milky and easier to slice and melt. The smoked version, affumicata, is traditionally smoked over burning straw, which gives it a golden rind and a gentle smoky aroma.

- Smoked provola is a classic partner for aubergine in parmigiana.

Ricotta is found all over Italy, but buffalo ricotta belongs to the same lands as buffalo mozzarella: the plains of Campania and southern Lazio, where water buffalo have been raised for centuries. Nothing was wasted in a dairy, and ricotta is the proof.

### How it is made
Ricotta is made from the whey left over after making cheese such as mozzarella. The whey is heated again until its remaining proteins rise to the surface as soft white flakes. These are gently lifted out and left to drain in baskets. Made with buffalo whey, ricotta is richer and creamier than the cow's-milk kind, with a delicate, sweet taste.

### Curiosities
- The name means cooked again, because the whey is heated a second time.
- Strictly speaking, ricotta is a dairy product made from whey rather than a cheese.
- Ricotta is the heart of many Neapolitan sweets, including the filling of sfogliatella and pastiera.

### How to enjoy it
Spread it on warm bread with a drizzle of honey or olive oil and black pepper. Use it to fill ravioli or cannelloni, stir it into hot pasta with a little cooking water, or fold it into a cake or a tart. It is best eaten within a few days.144:T4b7,### Waar het vandaan komt

Scamorza is a cheese of southern Italy, especially Campania, Molise, Abruzzo and Puglia. It belongs to the family of pasta filata cheeses, together with mozzarella, provola and caciocavallo, and for generations it has been a staple of kitchens across the south.

### How it is made
The curd is stretched in hot water until smooth and elastic, then shaped into a small pear. A string is tied around the top, creating the little head that makes scamorza easy to recognise, and the cheese is hung to dry for a short time. That drying makes it firmer and less milky than mozzarella. Scamorza comes in two styles: bianca, plain and mild, and affumicata, smoked, with a golden rind and a gentle smoky aroma.

### Curiosities
- The name is often linked to the southern expression capa mozza, cut-off head, from the way the top is pinched.
- Because it is drier than mozzarella, scamorza melts without releasing much water, which makes it ideal for cooking.
- Scamorza and provola are close cousins; scamorza is usually smaller and shaped like a pear.

### How to enjoy it
Cut it into thick slices and grill them in a hot pan until golden outside and soft inside. It is also excellent melted on pizza, in a panino, over roasted vegetables or baked with pasta. The plain one suits lighter dishes; the smoked one adds depth to potatoes and aubergine.14a:T5b2,### Waar het vandaan komt

### Hoe het gemaakt wordt
De wrongel wordt in heet water getrokken tot hij glad en elastisch is en daarna in de vorm van een peertje gebracht. Rond de bovenkant wordt een touwtje geknoopt, wat het kopje vormt waaraan je scamorza herkent, en de kaas hangt kort te drogen. Daardoor is hij steviger en minder melkachtig dan mozzarella. Scamorza bestaat in twee stijlen: bianca, naturel en mild, en affumicata, gerookt, met een goudkleurige korst en een zacht rokerig aroma.

### Weetjes
- De naam wordt vaak verbonden met de zuidelijke uitdrukking capa mozza, afgeknipt hoofd, naar de manier waarop de bovenkant wordt afgeknepen.
- Omdat hij droger is dan mozzarella, smelt scamorza zonder veel vocht los te laten; ideaal om mee te koken.

Prosciutto crudo, raw ham, is one of the great products of Italian charcuterie. It is made in many regions, and the most famous hams, such as those of Parma and San Daniele, carry protected designations. Wherever it comes from, it rests on the same simple idea: a pork leg, salt, air and time.

### How it is made
The fresh leg is trimmed and salted, then left to rest so the salt can slowly penetrate and draw out moisture. It is washed, the exposed meat is often protected with a layer of fat, and the ham is hung to age for many months in airy rooms. During that time the meat turns rosy and tender and develops its sweet, savoury flavour.

### Curiosities
- Crudo means raw: the ham is never cooked, only salted and aged.
- In Italy, asking simply for prosciutto usually gets you crudo; the cooked one is called cotto.
- The white fat is part of the pleasure: it carries much of the aroma.

### How to enjoy it
Take the slices out of the pack about ten minutes before serving and separate them gently. Serve them with melon or figs, drape them over focaccia or pizza after baking, or fill a panino with mozzarella. They are also lovely wrapped around grissini.14d:T4ee,### Waar het vandaan komt
Prosciutto crudo, rauwe ham, is een van de grote producten van de Italiaanse vleeswarenkunst. Hij wordt in veel regio's gemaakt, en de beroemdste hammen, zoals die van Parma en San Daniele, hebben een beschermde benaming. Waar hij ook vandaan komt, het idee is altijd hetzelfde en eenvoudig: een varkensbout, zout, lucht en tijd.

### Hoe het gemaakt wordt
De verse bout wordt bijgesneden en gezouten en daarna laten rusten, zodat het zout langzaam intrekt en vocht onttrekt. Hij wordt gewassen, het blootliggende vlees wordt vaak met een laagje vet beschermd, en de ham hangt vele maanden te rijpen in luchtige ruimtes. In die tijd wordt het vlees rozig en mals en krijgt het zijn zoete, hartige smaak.

### Weetjes
- Crudo betekent rauw: de ham wordt nooit gekookt, alleen gezouten en gerijpt.
- Vraag je in Italië gewoon om prosciutto, dan krijg je meestal crudo; de gekookte heet cotto.
- Het witte vet hoort erbij: het draagt een groot deel van het aroma.

### Zo geniet je ervan
Haal de plakjes ongeveer tien minuten voor het serveren uit de verpakking en maak ze voorzichtig los. Serveer ze met meloen of vijgen, leg ze na het bakken over focaccia of pizza of beleg een panino met mozzarella. Ook heerlijk gewikkeld om grissini.14e:T4f3,### Da dove viene
Il prosciutto crudo è uno dei grandi prodotti della salumeria italiana. Si produce in molte regioni e i prosciutti più famosi, come quelli di Parma e di San Daniele, hanno una denominazione protetta. Ovunque nasca, si basa sulla stessa idea semplice: una coscia di maiale, sale, aria e tempo.

### Come si fa
La coscia fresca viene rifilata e salata, poi lasciata riposare perché il sale penetri lentamente e richiami l'umidità. Si lava, la parte scoperta della carne spesso si protegge con uno strato di grasso, e il prosciutto viene appeso a stagionare per molti mesi in ambienti arieggiati. In questo tempo la carne diventa rosata e tenera e sviluppa il suo sapore dolce e sapido.

### Curiosità
- Crudo vuol dire che il prosciutto non viene mai cotto, solo salato e stagionato.
- In Italia, se chiedi semplicemente prosciutto, di solito ti danno il crudo; quello cotto si chiama cotto.
- Il grasso bianco fa parte del piacere: porta con sé gran parte del profumo.

### Come gustarlo
Togli le fette dalla confezione una decina di minuti prima di servirle e staccale delicatamente. Servile con melone o fichi, adagiale su focaccia o pizza appena sfornate o farcisci un panino con mozzarella. Sono ottime anche arrotolate intorno ai grissini.14f:T4b6,### Where it comes from
Mozzarella di Bufala Campana is a protected designation of origin. It may be made only from the milk of water buffalo raised in a defined area: mainly Campania, around Caserta and Salerno, plus parts of southern Lazio, Puglia and Molise. Buffalo have been part of these wet, flat landscapes for centuries.

### How it is made
The fresh buffalo milk is curdled and the curd is cut, rested and then stretched in very hot water until it is smooth and shiny. The cheesemaker shapes it and cuts it into pieces, then places them in a light brine. The DOP rules fix the area, the milk and the method, and every package carries the designation's mark.

### Curiosities
- The word mozzarella comes from mozzare, to cut off, the gesture used to separate each piece.
- Buffalo milk has more fat and protein than cow's milk, which gives the cheese its richness.
- Real buffalo mozzarella has a thin, shiny skin and releases a milky liquid when cut.

### How to enjoy it
Take it out of the fridge about half an hour before eating and keep it in its liquid until then. Tear it open and serve it with ripe tomatoes, basil and olive oil, with prosciutto crudo, or on top of pizza just after baking.150:T4d9,### Waar het vandaan komt
Mozzarella di Bufala Campana is een beschermde oorsprongsbenaming. Hij mag alleen gemaakt worden van melk van waterbuffels uit een afgebakend gebied: vooral Campanië, rond Caserta en Salerno, plus delen van Zuid-Lazio, Apulië en Molise. Buffels horen al eeuwenlang bij deze natte, vlakke landschappen.

### Hoe het gemaakt wordt
De verse buffelmelk wordt gestremd, de wrongel gesneden, laten rusten en daarna in heel heet water getrokken tot hij glad en glanzend is. De kaasmaker vormt hem, knipt hem in stukken en legt die in een lichte pekel. De DOP-regels leggen het gebied, de melk en de werkwijze vast, en elke verpakking draagt het merkteken van de benaming.

### Weetjes
- Het woord mozzarella komt van mozzare, afknippen, het gebaar waarmee elk stuk wordt losgemaakt.
- Buffelmelk bevat meer vet en eiwit dan koemelk, en dat geeft de kaas zijn volheid.
- Echte buffelmozzarella heeft een dun, glanzend velletje en geeft bij het aansnijden melkachtig vocht af.

### Zo geniet je ervan
Haal hem ongeveer een halfuur voor het eten uit de koelkast en laat hem tot dan in zijn vocht. Scheur hem open en serveer met rijpe tomaten, basilicum en olijfolie, met prosciutto crudo of op een pizza vlak na het bakken.151:T4e8,### Da dove viene
La Mozzarella di Bufala Campana è una denominazione di origine protetta. Si può produrre solo con latte di bufale allevate in un'area definita: soprattutto la Campania, intorno a Caserta e Salerno, più alcune zone del basso Lazio, della Puglia e del Molise. Le bufale fanno parte da secoli di questi paesaggi pianeggianti e umidi.

### Come si fa
Il latte di bufala fresco viene cagliato, la cagliata tagliata, lasciata riposare e poi filata in acqua molto calda finché non diventa liscia e lucida. Il casaro la modella, la mozza in pezzi e la ripone in un liquido leggermente salato. Il disciplinare DOP fissa zona, latte e metodo, e ogni confezione porta il marchio della denominazione.

### Curiosità
- La parola mozzarella viene da mozzare, il gesto con cui si stacca ogni pezzo.
- Il latte di bufala ha più grassi e proteine di quello vaccino, ed è questo a dare ricchezza al formaggio.
- Una vera mozzarella di bufala ha una pelle sottile e lucida e rilascia liquido lattiginoso al taglio.

### Come gustarlo
Tirala fuori dal frigo una mezz'ora prima e lasciala nel suo liquido fino al momento di servirla. Aprila con le mani e servila con pomodori maturi, basilico e olio, con prosciutto crudo o sulla pizza appena sfornata.152:T488,### Where it comes from
Stracciatella comes from Puglia, the heel of Italy, and in particular from the areas around Foggia and Andria, where it developed alongside burrata. It is the soft, creamy filling you find when you cut a burrata open, sold here on its own.

### How it is made
The cheesemaker stretches curd in hot water, as for mozzarella, then tears it by hand into thin strands. These strands are mixed with fresh cream until they become a loose, spoonable, milky mixture. There is no rind and no ageing: stracciatella is meant to be eaten fresh, within a few days.

### Curiosities
- The name comes from stracciare, to tear, after the torn shreds of curd.
- Stracciatella di bufala exists too, but the classic Apulian version is the one used in burrata.
- The stracciatella ice-cream flavour is unrelated: it is named after the shreds of chocolate in it.

### How to enjoy it
Spoon it straight from the tub onto warm focaccia or toasted bread with a drizzle of olive oil. Put a generous spoonful on top of a tomato pasta, a pizza fresh from the oven, grilled vegetables or a salad of ripe tomatoes. Take it out of the fridge a little before serving.153:T4a8,### Waar het vandaan komt
Stracciatella komt uit Apulië, de hak van Italië, en dan vooral uit de omgeving van Foggia en Andria, waar hij samen met burrata is ontstaan. Het is de zachte, romige vulling die je vindt als je een burrata opensnijdt, hier los verkocht.

### Hoe het gemaakt wordt
De kaasmaker trekt de wrongel in heet water, net als bij mozzarella, en scheurt hem daarna met de hand in dunne slierten. Die worden gemengd met verse room tot een losse, lepelbare, melkachtige massa. Er is geen korst en geen rijping: stracciatella is bedoeld om vers te eten, binnen een paar dagen.

### Weetjes
- De naam komt van stracciare, scheuren, naar de gescheurde slierten wrongel.
- Er bestaat ook stracciatella di bufala, maar de klassieke Apulische versie is degene die in burrata gaat.
- De ijssmaak stracciatella heeft er niets mee te maken: die is vernoemd naar de chocoladesnippers erin.

### Zo geniet je ervan
Schep hem zo uit het bakje op warme focaccia of geroosterd brood met een scheutje olijfolie. Leg een flinke lepel op pasta met tomaat, op een pizza vers uit de oven, op gegrilde groenten of op een salade van rijpe tomaten. Haal hem even voor het serveren uit de koelkast.154:T4a0,### Da dove viene
La stracciatella viene dalla Puglia, il tacco d'Italia, e in particolare dalle zone di Foggia e Andria, dove è nata insieme alla burrata. È il ripieno morbido e cremoso che trovi quando apri una burrata, qui venduto da solo.

### Come si fa
Il casaro fila la cagliata in acqua calda, come per la mozzarella, poi la sfilaccia a mano in strisce sottili. Queste si mescolano con panna fresca fino a ottenere un composto morbido, lattiginoso, da mangiare al cucchiaio. Niente crosta e niente stagionatura: la stracciatella va gustata fresca, entro pochi giorni.

### Curiosità
- Il nome viene da stracciare, per gli sfilacci di pasta filata.
- Esiste anche la stracciatella di bufala, ma la versione pugliese classica è quella che riempie la burrata.
- Il gusto di gelato stracciatella non c'entra: prende il nome dalle scaglie di cioccolato.

### Come gustarlo
Prendila a cucchiaiate direttamente dalla vaschetta e mettila su focaccia calda o pane tostato con un filo d'olio. Aggiungine una bella cucchiaiata sulla pasta al pomodoro, sulla pizza appena sfornata, sulle verdure grigliate o su un'insalata di pomodori maturi.
